  • Control (owed): the default sink is the base every swapped sink forwards terminal.* lines to (or the ledger writer is a separate, never-swapped channel), and a test asserts the full-run ledger balances; until then the ledger's unmatched count is read as "not recorded" for lines that fired under a swapped sink, never as a leak.
  • Status: uncontrolled — registered 2026-09-14; the measurement gap is named, the mechanism is not.

DC-221 — A drain whose exit condition is re-evaluated only when the thing it drains produces something can outlive it: the terminal signal completes after the last event and the loop waits for an event that never comes

  • Shape: await foreach (… in reader.ReadAllAsync(ct)) { …; if (prompt.IsCompleted && reader.Count == 0) break; } — a second completion source (the prompt task) is checked only inside the loop body, so when it completes after the last event nothing wakes the loop and the drain hangs until the queue closes for another reason.
  • Signature: a run that concludes in the engine but never in the thread; two identical read-only turns recording 11 vs 12 events (the drain exited at the answer frame in one and at a trailing usage_update in the other); a hang under a stub adapter that stops emitting.
  • Instance (2026-09-14, Ruling 95): GovernedRunHost.DrainAsync — found by the lane's concurrency measurement and the stub-adapter STA rows (docs/proof/send-while-running.md); fixed to end when the prompt is done and nothing is queued, in either order.
  • Sweep: grep -rn "IsCompleted && .*Count == 0" src/ — the one site, fixed.
  • Control: TheDrainEndsWhenThePromptEndsTests (3 rows: prompt completes before, after, and with no trailing event). The rule for the next drain: every completion source the exit depends on must be able to wake the loop (Task.WhenAny over the queue read and the signal).
  • Status: controlled.

DC-222 — An oracle reads a derived surface right after the source's condition became true, without letting the derivation run, and the failure reads as a wrong value rather than as staleness

  • Shape: await Until(() => readModel.X); Assert(rows[i].Y) with no pump between — the read model's condition holds, the derived row (a fold, a projection, a rendered item) has not yet been recomputed, and the assertion sees the previous value.
  • Signature: a row reading 0 events for a turn the read model had already concluded; flaky under load, green when stepped; the message names a value, not a timing.
  • Instance (2026-09-14, Ruling 95): the STA rows for the queued turn's drain read the fold before it pumped (docs/proof/send-while-running.md finding 2).
  • Sweep: the App tests' Until helpers (grep -rn "Until(" tests/AiDe.App.Tests); the pattern is the test idiom, not src/.
  • Control: the helper pumps after the condition holds (UntilAsync); an oracle over a derived row asserts after a pump, never directly after the source condition.
  • Status: controlled — a test-idiom class.

DC-223 — A launch resolver that composes a path without probing it lets "installed" be asserted by each caller's own probe, and one caller reads the wrong argument as the file

  • Shape: EngineCatalog.ResolveLaunch composes <root>/node_modules/<package>/<entry> (or a native command) and never touches disk, by design — its callers spawn the result and the spawn fails by name. Two other callers needed "is it installed?" and each probed File.Exists on its own reading of the launch: first use on the adapter entry; the New Session sheet on Arguments[0] — which for a Native row is --acp, not a file, so every native engine would have read not configured forever.
  • Signature: two File.Exists(launch.Arguments[0]) sites with different meanings; a native engine that is installed and on PATH shown as not configured.
  • Instance (2026-09-14): the accounts lane's finding (a) and its seam request req-01M2GQ9TCC65J8WAX0NZ55DA9X; the sheet's LaunchRefusal (NewSessionSheetViewModel.cs) and FirstUse.InstallAdapterAsync.
  • Sweep: grep -rn "Arguments\[0\]" src/ — the two sites, both repointed.
  • Control: EngineCatalog.InstallRefusal(engineId, root[, locator]) — the one derivation: an adapter's entry on disk, a native CLI's executable resolved (a bare PATH name vouched for by the locator, a path checked on disk), a row that cannot launch reading its launch refusal; TheCatalogKnowsWhatIsInstalledAndWhatModelTests (the native row's reading contains no --acp). ResolveLaunch stays pure.
  • Status: controlled.

DC-224 — A catalog row with no default model forces the first-use writer to name one in code: two homes for "the engine's model"

  • Shape: the file's engines.<id>.model is written by first use from a value the catalog row did not carry, so a switch in a dialog named the model per engine — the DM7 signature (two definitions of one quantity), with the wire-observed value cited in a comment instead of held by the row.
  • Signature: DefaultModelFor(engineId) => engineId switch { … } in a view; the row's fields end at the launch.
  • Instance (2026-09-14): the accounts lane's finding (b) and seam request req-01M2GQ9TF5T85K438JKWN8Q0DK; ConfigureProviderDialog.DefaultModelFor.
  • Sweep: the model's other readers (ProviderConfiguration.ReadEngines reads the file; the run request carries the bound model) — one producer once the row holds it.
  • Control: EngineRow.DefaultModel, set per row from the spike's observed currentModelId / availableModels with the citation on the row; the dialog reads the row; the theory EveryRowCarriesTheModelObservedOnItsWire pins the five values and fails on a new row that carries none.
  • Status: controlled.

DC-225 — A PATH resolver that tries the bare name first on Windows picks an extensionless POSIX script over its .cmd shim, and CreateProcess refuses it

  • Shape: node's global bin directory holds both npm (a POSIX shell script, no extension) and npm.cmd; a resolver that probes <dir>/<name> before <dir>/<name>.<PATHEXT> finds the script first, and CreateProcess refuses a file Windows cannot execute. Unit tests whose stand-ins were .cmd files never saw it.
  • Signature: "npm did not start: %1 is not a valid Win32 application" (or the equivalent) from a machine where npm --version works in a shell; green unit tests, red on the first real run.
  • Instance (2026-09-14): found by the fresh-machine oracle on its first run (Ruling 104 condition 2); FirstUse.Which fixed to prefer PATHEXT extensions on Windows; FirstUseTests.OnWindowsThePathResolverPrefersPathextOverAnExtensionlessScript.
  • Sweep: NativeCommandLocator (the engines lane's, EngineCatalog.cs:115-160) resolves with the same rule — read, and it probes extensions first on Windows.
  • Control: the named test; the rule for the next resolver: on Windows, PATHEXT candidates before the bare name.
  • Status: controlled.

DC-226 — A control shipped as a checked-in script runs at the version of whichever worktree invokes it, so a long-lived lane enforces the rules of the day it branched

  • Shape: a refusal, gate or driver lives in a file under version control; every session runs the copy in its own checkout; a lane branched days ago therefore enforces a superseded control set, and the fleet silently runs mixed versions of its own rules. Nothing compares the invoked copy against main.
  • Signature: a control that demonstrably exists on main does not fire for a peer; grep -c <the refusal's code> <other worktree>/…/<the script> returns 0; the lane's merge-base predates the commit that added it. The peer looks careless and is not.
  • Instance (2026-09-16): coord claim refuses register-class paths (COORD-CLAIM-REGISTER-CLASS, shipped in pack revision 70 on 2026-09-14, 0f553858), yet the Atlas native lane held a lease on docs/audit/audit-log.jsonl and stopped a main join at its commit step. Its own copy — C:/Projects/ai-de-atlas-e1-native-class-view/docs/ai-forward-pack/scripts/coord-core.py — contains zero occurrences of that code, while the recovery and five-gates trees contain one each. With 89 lane branches and 133 worktrees alive, the exposure is every control added this week.
  • Sweep: every control that ships as a repo file and is invoked per-tree — docs/ai-forward-pack/scripts/* and tools/*; the same shape applies to the merge drivers (they are configured per clone, and share the primary's .git/config, so those are safe) and to the gate runner.
  • Control: Ruling 131 (c) — coord claim and run-verify-gates.py compute whether origin/main carries commits touching docs/ai-forward-pack/scripts/ or tools/ since the invoking tree's merge-base, and refuse with the one-line remedy (merge origin/main); the named escape is an absent origin/main, which prints "control currency: not recorded" and proceeds, and escape use is written into the claim event so it is countable. Because a stale tree runs the stale check, coord worktree list run from main also reports currency per tree, so the watcher sees the fleet from a current copy.
  • Status: uncontrolled — the refusal is specified (Ruling 131 (c)) and not yet built; the fleet report is its companion.

DC-227 — A long-running control run under a harness mode whose termination status is the harness's, not the tool's

  • Shape: DC-113's generalisation, one step out: an exit code that has been through a pipe describes the last program in the pipe. Here the "pipe" is a background shell — the tool is started under a mode that reports its own view of the process, the tool's output is block-buffered so the last flushed line is several steps behind, and the reader concludes from both that the tool finished successfully at the step it last saw.
  • Signature: a tool whose source makes the observed ending impossible — an unconditional later step, a return 0 that only follows the final step — paired with a harness line like [exited with code 0] and a side effect that did not happen (nothing pushed).
  • Instance (2026-09-17, recurrence 4 of DC-113): conductor-join.py was run twice for real joins under this harness's background-shell mode. Both times the captured output ended at == step 7: commit; what the harness reported for each, verbatim, was [exited with code 0]; nothing was pushed. The source refutes the reading — step 8 is unconditional (:217-221), Join.run raises SystemExit(step) on failure (:126-129), return 0 follows step 10 (:237), and stdout is reconfigured for encoding only (:63-68). Whether the process itself exited 0 is not recorded. The conductor first reported this as "the join tool skipped its own gate", which was wrong in both halves and is corrected here.
  • Sweep: every long-running control this session starts — the join, run-verify-gates.py, the recount, dotnet test. The same reading error is available for each.
  • Control: Ruling 128 — conductor-join.py line-buffers its log and writes a per-step record to .agents/joins/<shortname>.json (step, command, exit code, timestamp) with a terminal complete record; a state file without `complete` is an unfinished join, machine-readably, and its self-test plants a killed step to prove it. Standing conductor rule until then: a join is never run where its exit status is unobserved; if backgrounded, the reader takes the recorded exit code, never the harness's status.
  • Status: partially-controlled — the standing conductor rule is in force (gates re-run bare, push explicit); the machine-readable state file is specified and not yet built.

DC-228 — CAP-P: a capability's presence on the host is inferred from the product's own configuration, or from a hardcoded list, instead of probed — and where a probe exists, its specific refusal is replaced by a generic absence

  • Shape: two sub-shapes. (i) A config guard short-circuits the probe: "we have no settings file, therefore we cannot know whether the tool is installed" — when the tool is on PATH and one call would say so. (ii) A requirement list is fixed in code rather than derived from the row's own declared requirement, so the thing that actually matters is never checked. Corollary: the product owns precise, actionable refusals and the operator is shown a generic absence instead.
  • Signature: a state sentence that is byte-identical across every item in a list, when the items have different install stories; a prerequisites block that ticks tools the engine does not use and omits the one it does; the literal string the operator quotes having exactly one producer, and that producer being a null-check rather than a probe; a comment three lines above the defect stating the correct rule.
  • Instance (2026-09-16, the operator's clean machine): all five accounts read "not configured (no adapter root — no provider file)" with copilot installed and on PATH. NewSessionSheetViewModel.LaunchRefusal (:404-407) returns that sentence whenever ~/.aide/providers.json is absent, short-circuiting EngineCatalog.InstallRefusal — the single installed-reading DC-223 created. Proven necessary and sufficient by a headless probe (one variable, both directions). Siblings, each separately blocking: ConfigureProviderDialog.cs:65-67 (fixed ["node","npm"] for every non-anthropic provider), :131-132 (sign-in hardcoded to claude-code, so "ready" is unreachable for github through the product — and its simplify: marker at NewSessionSheetViewModel.cs:477 had already fired), :151/:246 (a false "the adapter is not installed yet" for a native engine).
  • Why it survived — the field that matters: the PATH seam stops at EngineCatalog. Locator-taking overloads exist (EngineCatalog.cs:359, 379) but every caller above uses the no-locator overload, so no test above the catalog can control what PATH says — and the test discharging Ruling 105's condition 4 (TheSheetListsAccountsWithDerivedStatesTests.cs:53-57) asserts NotConfigured for exactly the input that produced the bug. The control certified the defect.
  • Direct parent: DC-223, which unified the installed-reading. CAP-P is the guard left standing in front of the unified reading — a repair that did not sweep its own frontier.
  • Control: thread NativeCommandLocator up to the state derivation (a prerequisite of any deterministic test, not a nicety); then a catalog-wide oracle: for every EngineCatalog.Rows entry × every provider-file state (absent · default root · override root), a synthetic PATH carrying the row's own launch artefact must never yield NotConfigured, and an empty PATH must yield the row's own cited refusal, never a generic one. Plus a prerequisite-derivation test (the dialog's tool list ⊇ the row's declared requirement) and a single-producer assertion (no launch-reason string literal outside EngineCatalog.cs). Each seen failing first: the oracle fails today on all five rows in the "absent" column.
  • Status: uncontrolled — INV-0013; the repair is Ruling 130's amendment to Ruling 104, UX lens before code.

DC-229 — A scope identifier stored where an artifact path belongs, so every consumer that resolves the path fails and one of them fails silently

  • Shape: a record's field means "the artifact's path relative to its scope"; a producer passes the scope's own id into it. Every downstream resolver then computes a path that cannot exist. One consumer reports the failure to the operator; another counts it as a skip and returns a confident, wrong, empty answer.
  • Signature: a user-facing "could not be located (X)" where X contains a : or otherwise is not a path; a guard that filters the field for empty rather than for shape; a skip counter with no reason code.
  • Instance (2026-09-16): TypeScriptExtractor.cs:781-785 and PythonExtractor.cs:404 pass request.ScopeId into Provenance(ArtifactPathId, …) (EvidenceAssertion.cs:25-30) and null for SourceLocation. StoreReader.DeclaringAssertion:163 filters artifact_path_id <> '' — non-empty passes — and ProjectionService.cs:1302-1309 renders the operator's exact sentence. The unreported half: SearchContent:1190-1265 resolves through the same call at :1212, so every TypeScript and Python file in every workspace is silently skipped and a content search returns "no matches" rather than "I could not open 1,100 files", for an unknown span of time.
  • Sweep (evidence, not assumption): all 19 new Provenance( sites in src/ read. Seven pass a real relative path (CSharpExtractor.cs:493 is the reference — path + line:col); three scope-summary rows are correct as written; two are wrong, and they are exactly the two extractors with no provenance assertion in tests (asserted only for C#, CSharpExtractorTests.cs:88, CallEdgeTests.cs:138). The gap and the defect are the same shape.
  • Marker note: all four simplify: markers in the subsystem sit on these two extractors and declare a ceiling on what is recognised — none declares that the shortcut also dropped provenance from file-grain to scope-grain. An undeclared cost of a declared shortcut.
  • Control: one parameterised headless test over every registered extractor: for each emitted assertion, File.Exists(Path.Combine(scopeDirectory, Provenance.ArtifactPathId)) — extractor-agnostic, so the tenth extractor is covered the day it is registered. Reinforced by a write-side invariant in StoreWriter rejecting an ArtifactPathId whose first segment carries a : (the scope-id signature), and by content.outcome tags plus a skip-reason breakdown so the silent half is measurable (INV-0014 P4). Note for the repair: Provenance is outside AssertionId, so the fix causes no id churn and no self-heal — existing stores keep the bad value until re-extracted.
  • Status: uncontrolled — INV-0014; P0's test must be seen red before the two-line fix.

DC-230 — A C# raw-string literal is invisible to BOTH of the project's UI lints, so anything written inside a hosted page survives every gate

  • Shape: a surface is authored as a page inside a string literal in host-language source. design-lint.py reads the design docs and ui-craft-gate.py reads the built source, and neither opens the string — so every rule both gates enforce is simply absent inside it. The defects that result look unrelated to each other (a layout bug, a palette bug) and share one cause: the medium is unread. Amended to this form by Ruling 142; the original statement named only the fixed extent, which is one instance of the blind spot rather than the class.
  • Signature: a pane whose chrome spans the full height while its primary content stops short; scaling arithmetic that predicts the drawn extent to the pixel; a constant appearing nowhere in any test; and — the tell that generalises — a value the design system already names, restated as a raw literal a few lines inside a `"""`.
  • Instance A — the fixed extent (2026-09-16): CanvasPage.cs:65 — #stage { height: 440px } inside a full-height Explore pane. Measured 1624×437 stage in a 1661×2002 pane: 21.8% of the height, 3.6% of the pane's area, ~72 px² per drawn node. fit()'s own arithmetic (437 − 100 + 18 = 355) predicts the measured 354 px disc, which is what proves the container rather than the content is at fault.
  • Why it survived: the value lives in CSS inside a C# string, invisible both to the XAML extent tests (CodingsLeftExtentTests.cs measures a composed visual tree) and to ui-craft-gate.py / design-lint.py (which read source and design docs). A blind spot between two gates — neither is broken, and nothing owns the gap. 440 appears nowhere else in src/ or tests/.
  • Sweep: the other inlined page strings on the same argument — src/AiDe.App/Web/composer.html, composer-host.html. SurfaceContentFactory.cs:661 (MaxHeight = 260 in a ScrollViewer) and :831 (MinHeight = 120) are deliberate caps and floors on lists, ruled out.
  • Instance B — the second palette (2026-09-17, measured): the same string restates the App's tokens as raw hex. Measured from the file, not estimated: 45 occurrences of 16 distinct colour literals (HTML numeric entities such as &#8962; excluded from the count). Seven are byte-for-byte restatements of App.xaml brushes — #12151A SurfaceBrush, #1A1F26 SurfaceRaisedBrush, #0D1014 SurfaceSunkenBrush, #2A313B BorderBrush, #E4E9EF TextBrush, #98A3B2 TextMutedBrush, #5B9DD9 AccentBrush — against DESIGN.md TC5, "hosted web surfaces receive the tokens; they do not restate them." The KG-visualization lens reported fifteen; the measured figure is sixteen distinct, and the measured figure is what stands.
  • Control (amended by Ruling 142 — the control is not replaced, and the distinction is the point): (i) the control is a headless contract test over every inlined page string, asserting that the region which is the pane's primary content declares no absolute `px` height and does declare a flex remainder with a min-height. Deterministic, no window, no WebView2. Seen failing first on height: 440px. Owed by Stream X phase P0b. (ii) the fix is token injection at navigate time (TC5), owed by the "Explore truthfulness" slice — after which (i) widens to assert that no colour literal appears in the string at all. P0b does not widen now: a control is something that fails when the shape recurs (CI6), and token injection alone does not fail on the next height: 440px somebody types — the assertion does. Widening the assertion ahead of the fix would not be red-first, it would be a permanently red gate.
  • Status: uncontrolled — stays uncontrolled until P0b lands (Ruling 142). INV-0014 P0/P1; state a frame-time budget as an acceptance criterion on the fix, since the stage grows ~20× in area and the canvas has never been measured at any size.

DC-231 — A test writes to a tracked file as a side effect, so a later control that requires a clean tree never runs — and the job reports a failure at a step that executed nothing

  • Shape: a test or helper appends to a file that is under version control, for a good reason of its own. Nothing fails. A later step whose first act is a clean-tree check then refuses to start, and the pipeline reports a failure at that step — a step which measured nothing. The reader sees a red control and concludes something about what the control tests. The control has no opinion: it never ran.
  • Signature: a failing step whose log shows a self-test passing and then a refusal; M <a tracked path> in that refusal; a failure that reproduces in CI and never locally; and a preceding step that is also red for its own unrelated reasons, offering a plausible wrong explanation ready to hand.
  • Why it survives — the field that matters: the topology differs between CI and a developer machine. The writer resolved its target through git rev-parse --git-common-dir, so on a machine with linked worktrees the write lands in the primary checkout while the lane under test stays clean. Every local run was clean, for as long as the defect existed. CI has exactly one checkout, so the tree it dirties is its own. A defect that is invisible in every environment but one is not rare — it is what a worktree-based fleet is for, pointed the wrong way.
  • Instance (2026-09-18): CI run 35290247518, job 105431315889, step 11 "Mutation replay — the controls can actually fail". mutation-replay --self-test: every guard fires, then REFUSING TO START — the tree is dirty. M .agents/requests.jsonl, exit 1. The writer was tests/AiDe.App.Tests/DesktopHold.cs:98, announcing Ruling 115 desktop holds into the primary's tracked coordination journal. The mutation set never executed, so nothing was learned about the controls' strength at that SHA. The conductor's own closing receipt (al-01M2RZA9ATJQNC48P6B05CADRV) attributed the job's failure to the App suite — true of a different step, and it described a known accepted population while a control silently had not run. Corrected by al-01M2TTK8HVW7ERBX1FWQZ1JP0E.
  • Second defect in the same file, found with it: every field of the announcement was hard-coded to one session — agent, from, session, the id prefix, the recipient, and a path naming a probe program unrelated to the run. So a desktop hold taken by any programme was recorded as one particular peer's. A ledger a fleet reads to find out who holds a shared resource, misattributing every entry.
  • Relation to DC-227: the same reading error one level out. There a harness's exit code stood in for the tool's; here a step's failure stood in for the job's, and the step that actually failed had executed nothing. A step's status is not a statement about its subject until you know the step ran.
  • Control: the rule coord itself uses — no session identity, no write. DesktopHold.Append returns without writing when AGENT_SESSION is unset, because a desktop hold serializes a fleet and where there is no fleet there is nothing to announce. Measured both directions on the real suite: AGENT_SESSION unset ⇒ 0 lines appended, so the tree stays clean and mutation replay starts; AGENT_SESSION set ⇒ 2 lines appended (START and END), so Ruling 115's serialization is intact. The stronger control, owed and not built: a CI step asserting the tree is clean immediately after the test step, so the dirt is attributed to the step that caused it rather than to the next one that trips over it.
  • Status: partially-controlled — the write is guarded and the attribution repaired; the attribute-the-dirt-to-its-cause check is owed.
